`BASE/results.json`, with the same original SP inputs, prompt and model settings.
The exporter reconstructs the base from its saved source experiments in memory,
checks all four export files against that reconstruction, retains its answers
and adds the completed new ones. First-generation exports without an explicit
replay descriptor are supported only when their protected sources identify one
unique combined experiment. New provenance records the experiment, source corpus
and base path for reproducible continuation. Duplicate compared pairs or case IDs
stop rather than choosing a replacement answer. The same joint conflict checks
apply to old and new answers; no source export or receipt is rewritten.

```bash
cd /home/dev/src/notedeck_worktrees/algos/oracle-mvp && \
python3 sse_multivector/experiment.py export-hierarchy \
  target/branch-comparison-v1/bcpv4-food-ancestors-v1/experiment \
  --base target/branch-comparison-v1/bcpv4-ancestors-v1/run \
  --source sse_multivector/hierarchy-refresh-v1 \
  --output target/branch-comparison-v1/bcpv4-food-ancestors-replay-v1
```

The output must be fresh and outside the source/model-experiment directories.
`results.json` retains the full graph, SP links, answer-bound proposals and
separate browsing links. `provenance.json` retains source hashes, the preparation,
answers/receipts/settings, original entries/SP links and masks. `summary.json`
reports mechanical checks, not semantic accuracy; `new_ancestor_links` counts
links added relative to the supplied base, or relative to SP links for the first
export. `ancestor_answers` and `comparisons` count all retained current answers.
`categories.json` reuses the
existing browser with individual masks, IDs, full post context and empty-mask posts:

```bash
cd /home/dev/src/notedeck_worktrees/algos/oracle-mvp && \
target/infomap-review-build/debug/sse-subject-navigation \
  --categories target/branch-comparison-v1/bcpv4-food-ancestors-v1/hierarchy/categories.json \
  sse_multivector/hierarchy-refresh-v1/masked-posts.json
```

Do not enable display-collapse options. Append `--check` for noninteractive
validation. No saved LG01 output is rewritten.

### Canonical hierarchy construction

Register only hierarchy runs the human explicitly designates as official. Use
one unique ID for the raw build and its derived menu/export in reports and
comparisons; checkpoints do not receive separate run IDs. **HR03** identifies
the official ASv6.100 50-note Bootstrap-v96 / Compare-v48 build. Its frozen inputs,
prompt/model settings, construction policy and native receipts are collected in
the [Markdown hierarchy register](../docs/recommendation-engine/hierarchy-runs.md).
Update that register and the explicit official-run list before regenerating the
HTML viewer with the offline
`catalogue-hierarchies` command. Diagnostic execution approval, completion and
browsing exports do not establish official status. The previous `H0001`–`H0701`
diagnostic inventory IDs are withdrawn and must not be reused.

The standalone canonical constructor is available through the shared launcher.
Oracle owns construction, comparison selection, consolidation and inspection;
the experiment runner owns execution, retries, caching and provider transport.
There is one construction algorithm, with no legacy policy switch. Read help
from a task worktree:

```bash
../experiment-runner/bin/experiment-runner prepare-canonical-traversal --help
../experiment-runner/bin/experiment-runner run-canonical-traversal --help
../experiment-runner/bin/experiment-runner advance-canonical-traversal --help
```

`prepare-canonical-traversal` accepts a schema-1 definition containing `corpus`,
saved `note_ids`, `local_schema_version`, `bootstrap_spec`, `comparison_spec`
and `consolidation_spec`. Current data must originate from ASv6.100 or an
explicitly approved later AS version. Prompt/schema files, corpus and constructor
source are frozen. After preparation, `run-canonical-traversal DIRECTORY
--attempt-budget PATH` runs construction unattended: it executes prepared native
experiments, collects their validated receipts, applies placements and prepares
the next work until stopping conditions pass or execution is blocked. Use an
existing persistent attempt allowance; every batch, warmup, retry and resume
shares that counter. Preparation and explicit advancement remain offline. The
automatic command calls the ordinary runner execution path under the task's
authorization and existing budget admission.

Compare-v179 uses symmetric fixed meanings: A is the incoming node and B is the
selected canonical node. The constructor supplies only subject and meaning to
this prompt, translates the two containment labels for traversal, and preserves
native answers and receipts unchanged. The existing local/canonical schema is
also supported. Graph contradiction checks apply to both formats; canonical
branches do not become extra fields in v179's model input.

Up to three local searches and a consolidation batch execute concurrently;
provider concurrency is controlled by the shared runner. Advance after each
batch finishes, without waiting for unrelated batches. Serialize placements and
permit only one automatic driver per construction directory. Resume by running
the same command with the same attempt-budget path. Exact saved results are
reused. The latest `run-summary.json` distinguishes graph status from execution
status and records blocked experiment paths and execution errors.

Native provider retries finish before their answers become construction results.
If allowance or pause interrupts an eligible retry, keep that case unresolved
for resume. Terminal failed or malformed answers hold the affected local entry;
other independent work continues. Failed and interrupted reservations remain
charged to the counter. An unfinished batch is never repeatedly relaunched by
the same invocation. Budget exhaustion, pause or infrastructure blockage drains
active work and returns a nonzero status with preserved partial results. Local
inventory exhaustion without the required note access and strict narrowing also
returns nonzero; it is not successful completion. Neither automatic execution
nor holds introduce an approval step for individual placements.

Graph state is saved atomically between advancements; each native receipt is
saved independently. After a process crash, rerun the same command: continue
from the saved graph and reuse completed requests. An interrupted request with
no saved result may need another attempt, without refunding its earlier
reservation. For an explicitly reviewed code fix, pass the existing
`--runner-upgrade-reason TEXT` option. It records old/new constructor source
hashes and the preserved graph hash, and forwards the reason to the native
runner's code-upgrade audit. It cannot authorize changing the corpus, prompts,
schemas or frozen experiment inputs. Finish active experiments before changing
their bound source. Saved meanings, decisions and native receipts are retained.

The current standalone constructor implements
[hierarchy generation V2](hierarchy-generation-draft-v2.md). It independently
establishes every original L1 meaning, then uses the unchanged PPv35 prompt to
score each meaning against its full post. PPv35 inherits Bootstrap runner
settings. All L1s scoring at least 50 are guaranteed; when none qualifies, only
the highest is guaranteed, with saved order resolving ties. Rotate across notes
and finish every guaranteed entry before considering completion. Failed scoring
is unresolved evidence, never zero or permission for a partial fallback.

For uncovered notes, process remaining L1s by prominence until each note is
admitted; permit only one such fallback search per note in flight. Next give
eligible local children matching existing canonical headings priority, including
children of one-note parents. Their original immediate parent must be admitted
and the matching canonical node must not yet represent that note. A heading
match is eligibility only; every selected child follows ordinary traversal.
When none qualifies, refine the largest unfinished multi-note canonical leaf
from the immediate children of its original supporting entries, then nearer
original descendants before farther ones. Use prominence within each note and
rotate across notes, without a deeper 50-point cutoff. Reassess after placement
and consolidation; process each original local entry at most once.

Up to three local searches can overlap on fixed graph snapshots; placements
follow selection order. Before placement, account for graph changes and reuse
only exact unchanged comparisons. Sharing a note does not exclude a canonical
comparison. An unchecked or contradicted mapped original-parent relationship
holds the entry without reopening a pruned branch.

The saved `menu` is separate from the raw comparison graph. For an equal-set
single-child parent, retain the deeper heading. For a parent with multiple
children, retain the parent and bypass equal-set children only on that browsing
branch, exposing their children through transitive containment. Display-only
`note_ids` preserve access through bypassed leaves without changing canonical
`entries`. The audit and requested viewer use the menu. No equal-note-set check
rejects an otherwise valid consolidation. Rebuild the menu from the original
graph as support grows.

Each selected local node receives an independently generated immutable meaning.
Every comparison is local versus canonical with fixed meanings; its input
fields follow the configured Compare contract. `neither` stops that branch; `uncertain` holds the
local entry. Containment and equivalence reuse established ancestor/descendant
relationships. Prospective placement retains cycle, returned-relation and
settled-answer checks. A canonical addition leaving six or more siblings triggers
consolidation. A proposed synthetic title/meaning must pass ordinary traversal
and grouping checks; null or rejection leaves the triggering placement intact.
Complete triggered consolidation before applying the next local placement.

Producer reviews remain bound to native input/output hashes. The latest user
verdict takes precedence over the latest agent verdict. A current rejection or
uncertainty at a receiving experiment or an exact reused producer holds the
entry. Output mechanics and graph checks do not establish semantic accuracy.
See the [V2 algorithm and preparation contract](hierarchy-generation-draft-v2.md).

Use these offline commands after construction:

| Command | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `audit-canonical-trial` | Check recursive distinct-note sets, coverage, note access, strict narrowing and sibling choices, with a finite work budget. |
| `shape-canonical-trial` | Retain selected saved nodes and witnessed ancestry, preserving meanings and supports in a fresh finished menu. |
| `export-canonical-viewer` | Export the saved menu and full original notes for the existing hierarchy review UI, only when requested. |
| `measure-canonical-candidates` | Evaluate comparison selection against validated saved construction snapshots. |
| `evaluate-canonical-policy-trial` | Compare bound native trials, generation usage and actual finished menus without repairing the candidate. |

Shaping can bypass redundant intermediates through witnessed transitive
ancestry. Omission is not a negative relationship or an equivalence merge.
Count original supporting notes plus recursive descendants, deduplicating note
IDs. V2 finishes after all guaranteed L1s and coverage work, eligible heading
matches, relevant multi-note-leaf descendants, placements and triggered
consolidations are settled. Every note must remain individually accessible and
every displayed edge must strictly narrow its distinct-note set. Exhausted
multi-note leaves may remain with direct note access; separate singleton
canonical nodes are not required. Unresolved scoring or missing coverage returns
an unresolved outcome, not successful completion.

Six siblings is a best-effort consolidation trigger, not a hard maximum. Null
or a semantically invalid proposal leaves the triggering placement intact;
an accepted synthetic addition can trigger another sibling check. There is no
additional requirement to find every possible regrouping or prove globally
minimal browsing depth. Offline tests exercise V2 through the native workflow.
The user-authorized [HR08–HR10 provider trials](../docs/recommendation-engine/hierarchy-runs.md)
have saved their initial L1 meanings and await a tested prominence-cache
eligibility fix before continuation. No completed hierarchy-quality
qualification or measured cost saving is claimed.
Keep finished menus out of subsequent raw-trial preparation.

Candidate ranking is a bounded scan of at most 4,096 local rows and 64 ancestor
headings. Comparison selection allows at most 512 eligible canonical nodes;
the policy evaluator bounds canonical states to 512 nodes/steps.
These are experimental limits, not evidence of release-scale feasibility.
Saved heading vectors must cover the nominated headings exactly; missing or
changed vectors fail rather than silently selecting a different policy.
`subject_matching_probe prepare --subjects` prepares receipt-bound standalone
heading vectors from a native subjects-only collection with `--worker-config`;
it requires no masks or deprecated vectors. `measure-subject-vectors` measures
saved loading/normalization, including optional read-only compact mappings,
without inference or overwriting reports.

Saved raw trials bind comparison-policy source paths as well as source bytes.
Running a frozen raw trial from a different project checkout can therefore
refuse with `canonical comparison policy changed`, even when the copied code
is identical. Keep the original checkout and bindings; do not rewrite saved
state or bypass validation. New trials prepared on `main` bind its own paths.
The saved shaped menu can be audited independently without moving the raw
construction checkpoint.

Integration verification covered 504 distinct test cases: 498 passed across
the initial suites and targeted reruns; six existing UI/artifact checks were
unavailable. The 29 initial failures were all confirmed 30-second fixture
timeouts during multi-phase continuation and passed with its 120-second test
deadline. No production guard was relaxed. The integrated launcher reproduced
the saved ASv6.100 fifty-note menu audit: 70 nodes, 74 strictly narrowing edges
and the same three sibling-limit violations. After synchronizing main, its
12 graph-search/shaping tests, all 40 selective-construction/comparison-guard
regressions and three shared-launcher/input-adapter checks passed on the combined
code. The opt-in graph-search path remains available without changing the
evaluated shortlist policy. No teacher calls were made.

### Native generation accounting

`summarize-experiments --spec SOURCE --output REPORT` verifies named saved
experiment groups and writes a fresh native report without inference. It
deduplicates original generation receipts across reused experiments, separates
fresh execution from original generation, and classifies cache warmups from
their original receipt-bound manifests. Warmups contribute to generation cost
without becoming comparison requests. Retries and failed attempts still count;
missing costs retain an unknown total and a known subtotal. An optional existing
attempt counter is checked under lock without refunds or resets.

The report uses `experiment-usage-summary-v3`. This corrects the earlier
cross-experiment warmup/comparison classification while retaining original cost
totals and exact reuse identity. Catalogue/API-equivalent cost is not a provider
invoice; do not treat reused generations as free or count reasoning twice.

### Collect saved answers into reusable inputs

`collect` is offline. It validates saved answers and receipts through the same
report loader as `show`, then writes a fresh output directory. Collection paths,
prompt versions, teacher settings and saved-answer reuse are data supplied to
`prepare`; no collection name, topic or post count is hardcoded into this path.

- Collect completed `subjects` experiments into `subjects.json`, retaining
  complete posts, IDs, source metadata, numbered SP entries and contexts. Supply
  that file to the existing `masks` or `grouped_masks` adapter.
- Collect completed `subject_pairs` experiments into `relationships.json`.
  Preserve exact template variables, all source evidence, split and reversal
  family IDs when supplied, five-way relation/reason answers, receipts and
  attributed reviews. Store each distinct prompt template once, with its hash
  and version IDs. Different experiments and prompt versions remain separate
  decisions; duplicate experiment/case records are rejected. Missing answers,
  unsupported labels/fields and changed sources prevent publication.
  Review verdicts and their current/stale bindings remain separate from the
  semantic relation. Collection assigns no accepted training status and does
  not repair reversal disagreements. Its provisional outputs still require
  training-role selection and semantic review; validation evidence is not TRAIN.
- Collect completed `masks` (word-position format) or `grouped_masks` experiments
  into `masked-posts.json`, `vector-map.json` and, when masks are nonempty,
  `worker-request.json`. Keep every original SP entry, including valid empty MP
  masks and selected posts with no subjects. Encode exactly identical nonempty
  source-byte ranges within one post once, retaining all subject associations.
- Collect completed `node_descriptions` experiments into `node-descriptions.json`.
  Each record preserves the supplied `node_id` and `node_title` and adds a
  nonempty description of at most 1,200 characters. Duplicate nodes are rejected;
  collection does not choose between trials or generate descriptions.
- Multiple experiment paths can combine disjoint collections or complete partial
  mask batches. Reject duplicate subject answers, conflicting posts, missing
  masks, invalid receipts and changed source data. Collection does not choose
  between repeated trials or treat generated semantics as user approvals.
- Each output includes `provenance.json` with source hashes, experiment settings
  and receipts. Outputs cannot overwrite existing directories or be placed
  inside source experiments. Their generated data is ignored from the outset.

For illustrative already-completed experiment paths:

```bash
cd /home/dev/src/notedeck_worktrees/algos/oracle-mvp
python3 sse_multivector/experiment.py collect \
  target/subjects-food target/subjects-music \
  --output target/combined-subjects
```

After separately authorized MP calls have completed, collection can also bind
an explicitly supplied SSE worker configuration to the new request:

```bash
cd /home/dev/src/notedeck_worktrees/algos/oracle-mvp
python3 sse_multivector/experiment.py collect \
  target/music-masks \
  --output target/music-sse-inputs \
  --worker-config sse_multivector/hierarchy-refresh-v1/worker-config.json
```

The generated worker configuration preserves the supplied model, checkpoint,
runtime paths and batch settings, updating only request/output paths and the
request hash. It does not run SSE or establish compatibility with existing
vectors; verify worker/runtime provenance before later encoding or reuse.
If every mask is empty, no worker request or configuration is needed.

This replaces experiment-specific assembly for new work. The historical
`hierarchy_refresh.py` preparation and saved HRv1 artifacts remain unchanged;
its mask-to-SSE conversion uses the same reusable implementation. Older
experiments without a saved dataset path can be imported using a current spec;
collection never reruns their answers. Source hashing and input assembly add
no model calls or cross-post comparisons. Collection reads each distinct source
once and hashes each result ledger once per validation pass. Existing SP/MP
request sizes and repeated post context remain unchanged.

### Subject, title, mask and prominence preparation

The user-approved [mechanical review rule](distillation-review-v1/README.md#mechanical-decisions-for-all-three-checks-october-9)
applies to DSM matching, DSS support and DSL relationships. Exact trusted teacher
subjects establish meaning matches and same-post support; exact ordered pairs
from actual trusted teacher links or numbered ancestor paths establish local validity. Identical parent and
child wording also preserves every parent restriction. Record such findings
without inference while keeping occurrence-specific coverage and placement.
Source-bound matching and support/relationship adapters now attach sufficient
evidence as `mechanical: {output, evidence}`. The shared runner validates this
output through the selected adapter and seals it with the source and prepared
inputs. Native reports expose `origin: "mechanical"`, `mechanical_evidence`,
`mechanical_sha256` and zero `attempts`, without a model receipt or usage.
Mechanical cases are complete at preparation and require no dispatch job, warmup
or request-budget allowance. Mixed runs dispatch only remaining semantic cases.
`prepare ... --repeat` bypasses this completion and forces real model requests;
the full prompt/config remain prepared. Normal saved-response reuse still applies
to semantic cases. Old frozen experiments without mechanical metadata retain
their recorded execution. Reports separate `mechanical_cases` from `model_checked`,
and matching call/token estimates exclude mechanical cases. No exact match leaves
a semantic question; conflicting expected labels remain visible and agent-assessed
expectations remain unverified. These changes do not qualify reviewers or run bulk grading.

`preview-distillation-review MANIFEST --model-output DIR [--model-output DIR ...]
--output FILE` counts all saved validation stages offline without creating
reviewer prompts or calls. It verifies frozen dataset bindings, model provenance,
sealed checkpoint metadata and production stage receipts, then reuses the
same teacher index to count mechanical and remaining semantic occurrences.
Reports preserve invalid and valid-empty outputs separately and union identical
semantic input groups across notes, versions and models. Matching groups include
the full post, ordered stable-ID teacher catalog and student wording; support
groups include post and wording; relationships use ordered parent/child strings.
These are distinct input counts under fixed prompts/settings, not actual prepared
request identities; saved-answer reuse, warmups and retries are not resolved.
Only evaluation reference content is decoded after all mixed-ledger identity
headers have been checked. Reserved files and checkpoint weights are not opened.
See the [complete validation preview and command](distillation-review-v1/README.md#complete-validation-workload-preview-october-9).

Add `--estimate` to count `o200k_base` rendered input-text tokens for the distinct
questions and all remaining occurrences; this requires `tiktoken` in the command's
Python runtime. Repeated `--timing-experiment EXPERIMENT` arguments add output-token
and runtime projections from verified fresh receipts with the same complete
approved template and Sol / xhigh settings. Warmup receipts are excluded from
verdict statistics; failures, retries and reused answers cannot enter the sample.
Output already includes reasoning. Reports retain cache-hit and prefix-role
strata, source bindings and sample sizes. Service work divided by eight occupied
slots is a projection, not measured batch wall time; median/p90 scenarios are
not confidence bounds. Input-text sizes exclude provider instructions, message
framing and schema overhead. Current automatic prefixes are below threshold;
separate natural-prefix opportunities do not select a cache mode or guarantee
hits. See the [current estimate and complete Nix command](distillation-review-v1/README.md#validation-review-token-and-runtime-estimate-october-9).

The experimental `prepare-distillation-coverage-search EVALUATION --output DIR`
command prepares approved **DCSv1** searches from completed native matching
and actual relationship checks. Repeated `--answer-id` flags select existing
answers. Preparation binds the existing selection, teacher-meaning assessments,
receipts and premise snapshots; it performs no inference. It omits no-match
and already-covered work, preserves matching-self guards, retains children
behind invalid, unrun or explicitly disputed edges, and shares exact candidate
wording/context duplicates. It also reuses accepted complete matching against
actual trusted teacher ancestor paths to settle positive placements before
search. Repeated `--premise-audit AUDIT.jsonl` arguments bind existing exact
input/output assessments when acceptance is separate from native reviews.
Current native acceptance also qualifies; stale, rejected, uncertain or missing
acceptance cannot license reuse. Available matching self guards remain enforced.
Evidence retains matching receipts, accepted audit hashes/lines or current
reviews, mechanical guard proofs and each actual teacher path/context.
Absent paths or null matches do not establish a negative. Ordinary `run`, `show` and `review` operate on the
returned experiments. Eligible repeated prefixes use `--prompt-cache`.
Complete negative evidence is mechanically validated; semantic correctness and
positive-witness verification remain separate requirements. Historical pair
agreement is diagnostic rather than qualification. This adapter does not replace
the deployed all-pairs evaluation or establish the eight-hour runtime target.
Its [four-note xhigh development trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#dcsv1-four-note-coverage-search-trial-october-11)
completes ten fresh searches plus four eligible warmups in approximately 75
seconds. Three complete-output audits accept all ten decisions and reasons;
unchanged DSLv10 verifies the sole witness through exact-input reuse. Native
`summarize-experiments` reconciles usage and the attempt budget. These small-panel
results do not qualify negative-search reliability or full-evaluation runtime.
The subsequent
[four-note positive-placement challenge](distillation-review-v1/README.md#dcsv1-four-note-positive-placement-challenge-october-11)
completes 38 searches and four eligible warmups in approximately 118 seconds.
All outputs are structurally valid; full-output audits record 34 accepted
outputs, two semantic errors and two uncertainties. Native
DSLv10 verification of all 18 witnesses uses 16 exact-input reuses and two
fresh calls; it rejects the invalid positive witness and accepts the other
17 pairs. One additional missed-witness evidence experiment reuses an accepted
pair with zero fresh calls. The final native summary reconciles 44 fresh
attempts, 17 reuses and no retries or failures; it keeps historical generation
usage separate. A false negative remains outside positive-witness verification.
The subsequently approved
[conditional matching/path reuse](distillation-review-v1/README.md#conditional-matchingpath-reuse-october-11)
is implemented in the experimental adapter. Offline native preparation settles
exactly four of these searches: 38 become 34, with all 89 self guards and
remaining reviewer inputs unchanged. It verifies matching receipts, audit
bindings and actual teacher paths without inference. Separate required-child
matching uncertainties remain unresolved. DCSv1 and full runtime remain unqualified.

Coverage preparation hashes each distinct receipt results file and manifest
once per snapshot binding pass, after native report verification. The hashes
are local to that snapshot; inherited source bindings do not replace fresh
reads, and the final preparation source recheck remains enforced. The
[four-note offline benchmark](distillation-review-v1/README.md#coverage-preparation-receipt-hashing-october-11)
reduces the slowest preparation from 13.25 to 7.61 seconds with exact case-input,
rendered-prompt, evidence and protected-source parity. Eighteen targeted tests
pass, including changed-receipt rejection. No inference or review criteria
change; the full-evaluation runtime target remains unqualified.

The [focused DCSv1 fresh repeat](distillation-review-v1/README.md#dcsv1-focused-negative-search-controls-october-11)
selects twelve unchanged residual cases with native `prepare --from --only
--repeat --prompt-cache`. Independent expected judgments are recorded before
calls. Generation finishes in 72.36 seconds; all eleven unchanged expectations
pass full-output audit, and one mistaken null expectation is corrected without
declaring a model error. All seven returned witnesses receive unchanged DSLv10
verification through exact response reuse. The completed verification subsets
retain the original history root that indexes those receipts; an alternate root
can miss reuse even with identical input strings. Native accounting records
sixteen fresh attempts including four warmups and seven verification reuses.
The historical provisional uncertainty remains alongside explicit subsequent
acceptances; this test changes no review-precedence semantics and does not
establish broad reviewer qualification.

The [four additional-note coverage trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#dcsv1-four-additional-notes-october-11)
adds 24 new DCS inputs from the frozen sixty. Candidate preparation now honors
exact input/output/check-bound external actual-edge disputes, preserving the
existing native-review veto and otherwise-positive omission behavior. This
corrects the approved unresolved-edge rule; it adds no acceptance requirement.
The native regression first fails, then all twenty relevant tests pass. Three
disputed edges are retained before their five affected inputs run; only one
rendered input changes, while the other twenty-three retain exact parity.
Twenty-two complete search outputs are accepted and two remain unresolved.
Both returned witnesses reuse unchanged DSLv10 responses. The gas-fee/Payments
pair has conflicting search and verification results; neither model agreement
nor the verifier Boolean establishes semantic correctness. The original two
experiment worktrees remain frozen, with integration in a separate checkout.
Native accounting records 29 fresh attempts including five warmups, two
verification reuses, no retries or failures, and 28.27% fresh cached input.
Prompts, effort settings, review precedence and final qualification remain
unchanged; the full-evaluation runtime is still unqualified.

The [focused payment diagnostic](distillation-review-v1/README.md#payment-type-disagreement-diagnostic-october-11)
uses native exact fresh DCSv1/DSLv10 repeats and four pre-assessed authored
DSLv10 controls. DCS again selects Payments; unchanged DSL flips its earlier
rejection to acceptance. All four control decisions and explanations pass,
and a source-only trusted training Payments/price edge subsequently supplies
the category interpretation for accepting both exact outputs. The earlier
DSL charge/rate exclusion is too narrow. Final agent acceptances are appended
without discarding provisional uncertainty or changing review precedence.
Native accounting
validates seven fresh calls including one eligible DCS warmup, no reuse,
retry or failure, and a 58.39-second generation span. Prompts and source
bindings remain unchanged; no new semantic rule or prompt edit is adopted,
and further votes do not supply semantic ground truth. Supplying the trusted
example to both prompts is subsequently authorized and tested below.

The [trusted payment example trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#trusted-payment-example-prompt-trial-october-11)
added identical source-grounded precedent to experimental **DCSv2/DSLv11**
through pinned native revisions. Six fresh complete outputs pass full-output
agent audit; exact inputs, evidence, settings, schemas and cache boundaries
retain parity. The DCS prefix grows to 1,285 tokens with a new native key; DSL
remains uncached. Generation takes 49.47 seconds. Native accounting records
eight attempts including warming and one recovered socket failure, with no
reuse or unfinished case. Missing failed-call usage keeps total API-equivalent
cost and aggregate cache savings unknown; known subtotal is $0.0866192.
DCS's one-case warming adds $0.0029972 in net estimated cost. Production
defaults and broader qualification remain unchanged. The experiment worktree
is retained for source bindings; NGP/Investments interpretation remains open.
Those example-bearing versions are subsequently **withdrawn**: the unchanged
baseline already passed the same six exposed cases, so no benefit was shown.
Their withdrawn current prompt/revision files are removed; frozen experimental
sources and receipts remain intact.

The [fresh development trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#fresh-development-after-example-withdrawal-october-11)
uses unchanged DCSv1/DSLv10 as experimental baselines and selects validation
posts outside the original sixty's 54 note IDs and maintained development
definitions. The small DSL trial uses existing source-bound `distillation_verdict`
schema-2 actual-edge preparation; it tests the common AS relationship rule,
without claiming DCS search/coverage-workflow validation or inventing missing
matching premises. Independent pre-call labels and unresolved interpretations
stay separate. The exposed original sixty remain regression/workload evidence;
a later unseen-note accuracy assessment requires separately reserved cases.
The completed source-bound DSLv10 trial contains ten actual pairs on four
posts outside both exclusion sets: six frozen positive expectations, two
negative expectations and two uncertainties. All eight resolved decisions and
complete explanations pass independent audit; uncertainties remain unscored.
Native accounting includes only the final experiment: ten fresh calls,
106.45 seconds, no reuse/retry/failure/warming/cache hit, and complete
API-equivalent estimate $0.164466. Its natural prefix has 76 tokens, below the
warming threshold. Prompts, schemas and production code remain unchanged;
the frozen task worktree is retained. DCS null-search qualification still
requires separate testing with its genuine matching/self-guard prerequisites.

The subsequent [native coverage diagnostic](distillation-review-v1/README.md#native-coverage-diagnostic-with-genuine-prerequisites)
freezes actual saved answers and pre-call assessments, runs complete matching
and necessary self guards through native groups, then searches residual
candidates. Seven source-null searches and one positive Songs-beneath-Music
control pass independent full-output audit; unchanged DSLv10 separately verifies
the positive pair. Original premise ledgers remain frozen; verification uses
a separate native experiment. Support and unrelated relationship checks stay
unrun and cannot license omissions. Native accounting includes 22 fresh
attempts, one warmup, six duplicate occurrence reuses, no failures/retries or
missing usage, and $0.17604 API-equivalent cost. Warming produces no cached
tokens and adds $0.00495. Source selection and auditing between stages account
for much of the 724.576-second first-to-last run span; the union of native run
intervals is 150.743 seconds. N13 is previously DSL-exposed; N53 excludes that
four-post trial. Prompts, schemas and production behavior stay unchanged.
One positive control does not qualify diverse-data false-null reliability,
minimum reasoning effort or full-evaluation runtime. Frozen artifacts retain
the task worktree. The subsequent [high comparison](distillation-review-v1/README.md#high-comparison-on-the-frozen-coverage-controls)
reuses the same eight DCS cases and one DSL verifier with native fresh calls.
All nine complete outputs pass independent audit without observed substantive
degradation. Three groups run concurrently in 23.106 seconds; matched native
cost falls from $0.101884 to $0.062544 (38.61%), with identical input tokens,
no cached tokens and one warmup in each run. There are no retries, failures,
reuse or missing usage. Matching premises remain at xhigh/mechanical settings;
this small mixed-control comparison does not qualify minimum effort across
all checks. The subsequent [medium comparison](distillation-review-v1/README.md#medium-comparison-on-the-frozen-coverage-controls)
also passes all nine full-output audits, using the same inputs, prompts and
cache policy. Its three concurrent groups finish in 19.054 seconds; ten fresh
attempts cost $0.055674, 45.36% below matched xhigh and 10.98% below high.
There are no retries, failures, reuse, missing usage or cached tokens. Medium
passes this limited panel; the following low comparison completes the effort
descent before expansion. Matching/support and broader qualification remain
open, and production defaults stay unchanged. The subsequent [low comparison](distillation-review-v1/README.md#low-comparison-on-the-frozen-coverage-controls)
also passes all nine full-output audits and all 44 candidate rejection reasons.
Three concurrent groups finish in 18.087 seconds; ten fresh attempts cost
$0.054944, 46.07% below matched xhigh and only 1.31% below medium. Inputs and
cache policy remain identical; cached tokens, retries, failures, reuse and
missing usage remain zero. Low is the lowest passing level tested on this
limited panel. The subsequent [four-note diagnostic](distillation-review-v1/README.md#four-note-expansion-and-step-back-to-medium)
reuses twenty established source judgments on four saved answers: twelve
coverage searches, seven DSL witnesses and one actual missing-restriction edge.
Low passes 19/20 but falsely rejects Peacock beneath Television, adding a
television-content/activity requirement absent from the broad parent. Its five
percentage-point loss exceeds the approved two-point allowance. Under the
standing policy, the same cases immediately run at medium and pass 20/20 full
output audits. A weak illustration in one otherwise-supported negative is
explicitly caveated. Each level sends 23 fresh attempts including three warmups,
with no retries/failures/reuse/missing usage. Concurrent spans are 27.871/26.054
seconds and API-equivalent costs $0.1207736/$0.1286736 for low/medium. Both have
4,096 cached input tokens; warming outweighs the observed cache savings.
Source snapshots and original expectation metadata stay frozen; interpretation
judgments use the documented corrected source labels. This targeted regression
does not evaluate every subject or retest matching/post support. The following
[matching/support comparison](distillation-review-v1/README.md#medium-matching-and-support-on-the-same-four-answers)
does test every occurrence of those two checks on the same four saved answers.
Native routing handles 58 decisions mechanically and 96 through duplicate
reuse, leaving ninety fresh semantic calls plus one eligible warmup. Medium
support passes 45/45 resolved decisions; matching passes 39/40, excluding five
pre-existing ambiguous interpretations. Posting falsely returns null despite
its established Post sharing interpretation. Matching's 2.5-point loss exceeds
the approved two-point allowance and triggers the standing high step-up.
Medium runs complete in 24.625 seconds, with no retries/failures/missing usage
and $0.1937144 API-equivalent cost; warming-inclusive cache savings are 10.62%.
The [high matching repeat](distillation-review-v1/README.md#high-matching-step-up-on-the-same-four-answers)
fixes Posting and retains all 40/40 resolved decisions in 25.040 seconds,
using forty-five calls plus one warmup and $0.1191624 API-equivalent cost.
There are no retries/failures/missing usage; net cache savings are 9.05%.
Five interpretation uncertainties and three correct-null explanation caveats
remain visible in native reviews. High matching and medium support are
expansion candidates, not a globally qualified minimum.
The [complete four-answer trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#complete-four-answer-review-and-effort-step-ups-october-11)
reuses those matching/support results and finishes the necessary actual-edge,
guard and coverage work. Medium rejects a valid human-family Mothers edge;
high corrects it. Medium DCS selects an invalid Incest witness, caught by high
DSL, and a fresh repeated medium call misses Peacock's valid Television match.
High final coverage restores that match. Its thirty resolved searches are
correct, with four source interpretations unscored; all selected witnesses
already have exact high DSL outputs. The tested expansion candidates are now

usage is unknown. Final high DCS takes 71.636 seconds, separate from preparation
and audit time. Prompts, schemas and production defaults remain unchanged.

The authorized [complete-answer reviewer trial](distillation-review-v1/README.md#complete-answer-reviewer-trial-october-9)
adds native project commands: `prepare-distillation-review-trial`,
`show-distillation-review-trial`, `assess-distillation-review`,
`freeze-distillation-review-assessments`, `prepare-distillation-review-evaluation`,
`run-distillation-review-evaluation` and `show-distillation-review-evaluation`.
The `distillation_review_evaluation` adapter retains complete saved validation
answers and all numbered occurrences, routes trusted exact results mechanically,
and delegates requests, reuse, warmups, retry handling and receipts to the shared
runner. Compatible-heading comparisons use the configured relationship template,
defaulting to DSLv2; required flags and meaning groups come from a source-bound
candidate-independent assessment.
Evaluation preparation's optional `--matching-reasoning-effort` overrides
matching alone while `--reasoning-effort` supplies the other check settings.
Coverage-search preparation also accepts `--reasoning-effort`, retaining
xhigh as its default. This lets new native preparations bind the tested
high matching / medium other-check combination and reuse exact saved requests
without changing source wording, mechanical rules or existing manifests.
Expectation files freeze before calls and remain agent-assessed rather than gold.
Only repeated natural prefixes meeting the existing eligibility threshold are
warmed, using explicit prefix/key configuration. Sequential native groups use
the shared host queue and share a new-attempt allowance. Historical selection
concurrency remains metadata; new preparation emits no concurrency control. New schema-2
evaluations stop questions that cannot change the score: absent full matches, placements
already containing a match, a known compatible uncovered placement, and incoming
edges of unsupported subjects. Matching-self compatibility guards remain
required. The runner selects frozen case IDs with `only`; the evaluator warms
each eligible prefix at most once per invocation/group across adaptive waves.
Native reports assemble the
existing scores and compare them with frozen expectations, keeping unanswered or
ambiguous work visible. Use `--output FILE` on the show command to retain the full
report; terminal output contains its summary and usage.

The original trial completed DSMv3/DSSv2 before DSLv2 was paused at the user's
request to investigate scalability. Raw
semantic agreements are 569/591 and 585/600 resolved distinct inputs; unresolved

`review --role agent` entries record independent disagreement audits without
changing frozen labels. Four clear errors remain in each completed check, and

reports 42.14% cached comparison input; support reports none. One transport
attempt was retried successfully but has unknown usage: native accounting keeps
complete cost unknown and counts original receipt lines, warmups and reuse
separately. See the trial record for the completed-check evidence and remaining
relationship work. The coordinator drained already-submitted workers and exited;
no worker remains. The original paused native report counts 2,115 original receipts,
including nine warmups, four usage-unknown recovered failures and two extra
successful relationship dispatches for identical inputs. There are 870 distinct
successful relationship inputs: 808 fully joined, 62 further reusable inputs and
9,664 requiring new model work. Coverage adds 9,678 requests to the selected
trial's 2,086 primitive checks and was excluded from the older runtime estimate.

while new selections include ancestor proofs. Preparing a separate evaluation

results with no new inference. See the

at xhigh, then screening high/medium/low on a small paired subset and expanding

The current shared runner uses automatic queue admission; inherited concurrency
fields remain metadata. See the
[case-level process and prepared reference](distillation-review-v1/README.md#case-level-prompt-development-approved-october-10).
Prepare a small plan with `prepare --from OLD --only CASE_ID` before iterating;
`run --only` filters dispatch but retains the large group's full report.
The initial subset preparation still reports OLD once. Future candidate revisions
inherit the small plan, complete inputs, schema and source bindings, select the
current shared worker, and use `--prompt`/`--prompt-id` without `--repeat`.
Only intentional fresh trials of unchanged instructions use `--repeat`.
Inspect cache eligibility again when instructions change; preserve eligible
natural prefixes and do not pad or warm short ones. Audit decisions and reasons
against separate source assessments, not legacy qualification labels alone.
`prepare-distillation-review-evaluation --reasoning-effort high|medium|low|xhigh`
freezes an override in the native request settings without changing the source
selection. Without the flag, the selection's effort is retained.
Repeat `prepare-distillation-review-evaluation --answer-id ID` to freeze only
the named answers and scope the report, preserving their complete native cases
and original source bindings. Unknown or duplicate IDs fail before output
creation; omitting the flag retains the full selection. The removed
`--concurrency` preparation flag is not supported by the current shared runner.
`--relationship-prompt PATH --relationship-prompt-id ID` binds an alternate
relationship template during the same native preparation. Both flags are
required together; natural-prefix grouping and generated specs use that
template. Matching, support, schemas, source assessments and mechanical rules
are unchanged. Omitting the flags retains DSLv2. Compatible saved receipts
remain reusable; do not use `--repeat` for a prompt update.
`--relationship-runner-binary PATH` overrides only the relationship worker;
other checks retain `--runner-binary` or the default. Worker identity remains
part of semantic request identity. This allows unchanged completed checks to
retain their original worker binding while a new relationship trial uses the
current worker, without modifying receipts or relaxing reuse validation.
`--relationship-history-root PATH` selects saved-request history only for
relationship checks; matching and support retain `--history-root`. Omitting
the override preserves the existing common history. See the
[four-note preparation](distillation-review-v1/README.md#four-note-native-preparation-october-11)
for the scoped regression and verified original-worker reuse.
Repeat
`run-distillation-review-evaluation --answer-id ID` to dispatch a nested subset
of stable source answer IDs; reports retain the prepared answer scope, with
unselected answers pending. Unknown or duplicate IDs fail before dispatch.
Omitting `--answer-id` retains the complete-answer workflow. For fixed diagnostic
inputs, prefer a small native preparation as above; selecting dispatch inside a
large group does not shrink its report. A diagnostic subset supplements the
complete workflow without changing stopping rules or frozen source labels.

The core workflow is task-independent. Optional adapters use the existing
production preparation and decoding functions:

- `dataset.adapter: "distillation_review"`: source-bound
  [DSRv1 qualification definitions](distillation-review-v1/qualification-source.json)
  select complete frozen AS examples or a saved training control. Preparation
  preserves the full task/post/teacher, applies specified candidate changes and
  supplies all exact subject matches. Expected answers remain outside the prompt.
  The report validator checks complete subject/link verdicts, teacher partitions,
  fixed requirement flags and known references before calculating correctness
  and coverage. Duplicate coverage references are checked in application code;
  the provider response schema omits unsupported `uniqueItems`.
  `show --json` includes qualification checks after saved results.
  The October 8 DSRv1 trial fails known semantic and cross-version checks despite
  complete large reports; one stream is interrupted near fifteen minutes and
  remains ungraded. All 29 cases were attempted once, with 28 complete reports;
  seven of the eight largest generations completed. The maintained specification
  uses user-directed concurrency 8 with existing backoff; the original frozen
  trial used 1. See the
  [DSRv1 qualification evidence](distillation-review-v1/README.md#october-8-qualification-evidence).
  The current specification prepares
  [DSRv2 preview](distillation-review-v1/README.md#dsrv2-draft-and-offline-preview).
  A reference may supply `teacher_meanings_from` with `experiment`, `case_id`
  and `record_sha256`. Native report validation must yield a valid review of
  the same task, post and teacher; preparation pins the receipt and protects its
  experiment/configuration/manifest/ledger hashes. Only meaning groups and
  requirement flags enter the candidate input; their receipt provenance remains
  review evidence. Shared saved groups cannot be overridden by a candidate and
  are not promoted to known gold. Q20–Q22 use provisional groups for consistency
  checks. Each report's prompt ID must match its rendered reviewer version.
  Preparing DSRv2 adds no inference or authorization for calls.
  The user subsequently authorized DSRv2 qualification in small-control then
  large-case order. Q04 passes `8/8` correctness and `2/2` coverage, with both
  meanings required; its exact receipt is reused for the small set. Seventeen
  controls pass, Q14 fails semantically, and Q01's HTTP 200 body stream closes
  with an ungraded partial report after 365.2 seconds. Qualification has failed;
  Q20–Q29 finish separately at concurrency 8 without resending Q01: six complete
  reports (four unchanged large cases and both variants) and four ungraded body
  socket interruptions, without retries. Both variants preserve supplied groups
  and required flags but fail cross-version consistency on untouched links and
  compatible headings. Native `show --generation-costs` counts Q04's original
  call once and marks all five interruptions' final usage unavailable in their
  respective subset reports; SDK zero placeholders are not measured zero usage.
  The large subset retains the full case catalogue, so small cases appear
  pending there and must be inspected in their completed small subset. See the

  subjects-only pilot's prepared hashes and split identities, sizes every saved
  hierarchy, and returns the union of the top five training answers by entry

  sizes only; the output contains training references without posts or subjects.
